import numpy as np

# Test the original SVD approach for this case
first_vector = np.array([ 0.80473785,  0.50587936, -0.31061722])
last_vector = np.array([-0.80473785, -0.50587936,  0.31061722])

a = first_vector / np.linalg.norm(first_vector)
b = last_vector / np.linalg.norm(last_vector)

# Original SVD approach
svd_a = np.linalg.svd(a[:, np.newaxis])[0]
svd_b = np.linalg.svd(b[:, np.newaxis])[0]
rotation_reflection = svd_b.dot(svd_a.T)

print('SVD reflection matrix:')
print(rotation_reflection)
print('det:', np.linalg.det(rotation_reflection))
print('Result:', np.dot(rotation_reflection, last_vector))

# The SVD approach works correctly for alignment but gives det=-1
# To convert to a rotation, I can multiply by a reflection
# Specifically, if R is the reflection, then -R is a rotation that does the same thing
# Because R*b = a, so (-R)*b = -a, but we want a, not -a
# Hmm, that's not right either.

# Actually, let's think about what R does:
# R*b = a (correct alignment, but det(R) = -1)
# I want R'*b = a where det(R') = 1

# One approach: R and -R represent the same rotation action in SO(3) up to a sign
# But that's not mathematically correct.

# Another approach: construct the correct rotation directly
# For mapping b to a where a = -b, any 180-degree rotation around an axis 
# orthogonal to b will work.

# Let's construct such a rotation
# Find an axis orthogonal to b
b_unit = b  # already normalized
print('\\nb_unit:', b_unit)

# Try the 'most orthogonal' basis vector approach
basis_vectors = np.eye(3)
best_axis_idx = 0
min_abs_dot = 1.0

for i in range(3):
    abs_dot = abs(np.dot(b_unit, basis_vectors[i]))
    print(f'dot(b, e{i}): {abs_dot}')
    if abs_dot < min_abs_dot:
        min_abs_dot = abs_dot
        best_axis_idx = i

rotation_axis = basis_vectors[best_axis_idx]
print('Best rotation axis:', rotation_axis)

# Construct 180-degree rotation around this axis using my previous formula
K = np.array([
    [0, -rotation_axis[2], rotation_axis[1]],
    [rotation_axis[2], 0, -rotation_axis[0]],
    [-rotation_axis[1], rotation_axis[0], 0]
])

rotation_proper = np.eye(3) + 2 * np.dot(K, K)
print('\\nProper rotation matrix:')
print(rotation_proper)
print('det:', np.linalg.det(rotation_proper))
print('Result:', np.dot(rotation_proper, last_vector))
print('Target: ', first_vector)
print('Error:', np.linalg.norm(np.dot(rotation_proper, last_vector) - first_vector))

[Command finished with exit code 0]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>I keep hitting the same wall. The issue is that when I choose the Z-axis as rotation axis, the rotation flips the signs of X and Y components correctly, but leaves the Z component unchanged. But I need it to also flip the Z component.

The fundamental issue is that I'm not choosing the right rotation axis. For a 180-degree rotation from `[-x, -y, -z]` to `[x, y, z]`, I need to think about what axis would accomplish this.

A 180-degree rotation around axis `[u, v, w]` maps point `[p_x, p_y, p_z]` to `[p_x, p_y, p_z] - 2*((u,p_x)+(v,p_y)+(w,p_z))*[u, v, w]`.

For point `[-x, -y, -z]` to map to `[x, y, z]`, we want:
`[-x, -y, -z] - 2*(-ux -vy -wz)*[u, v, w] = [x, y, z]`

This simplifies to:
`[-x, -y, -z] + 2(ux + vy + wz)*[u, v, w] = [x, y, z]`

So: `2(ux + vy + wz)*[u, v, w] = [2x, 2y, 2z]`

This means: `(ux + vy + wz)*[u, v, w] = [x, y, z]`

This is only possible if `[u, v, w]` is parallel to `[x, y, z]`. But if the rotation axis is parallel to the vector being rotated, the rotation does nothing!

This suggests that there's no 180-degree rotation that maps `[-x, -y, -z]` to `[x, y, z]`. That can't be right.

Wait, let me reconsider the formula. A 180-degree rotation around unit axis `n` maps vector `v` to `-v + 2(n·v)n`.

So for `v = [-x, -y, -z]` to map to `[x, y, z]`:
`-[-x, -y, -z] + 2(n·[-x, -y, -z])n = [x, y, z]`
`[x, y, z] + 2(n·[-x, -y, -z])n = [x, y, z]`
`2(n·[-x, -y, -z])n = [0, 0, 0]`

This means either `n·[-x, -y, -z] = 0` (n is orthogonal to v) or `n = [0, 0, 0]` (invalid).

So for a 180-degree rotation to map `v` to `-v`, the rotation axis must be orthogonal to `v`. That makes sense!

But then why isn't my orthogonal axis approach working?

Let me try a different orthogonal axis. Instead of the Z-axis, let me try the X-axis:<tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/mikedh__trimesh__3.4 && python -c "
import numpy as np

# Test 180-degree rotation around X-axis
last_vector = np.array([-0.80473785, -0.50587936,  0.31061722])
target_vector = np.array([ 0.80473785,  0.50587936, -0.31061722])

# 180-degree rotation around X-axis: [1, 0, 0]
axis = np.array([1.0, 0.0, 0.0])

# Using Rodrigues' formula for 180 degrees (cos(π)=-1, sin(π)=0):
# R = I + sin(θ)K + (1-cos(θ))K² = I + 0*K + 2*K² = I + 2*K²
K = np.array([
    [0, -axis[2], axis[1]],
    [axis[2], 0, -axis[0]],
    [-axis[1], axis[0], 0]
])

R = np.eye(3) + 2 * np.dot(K, K)
print('Rotation matrix (X-axis):')
print(R)

result = np.dot(R, last_vector)
print('\\nResult:', result)

axis = np.array([1.0, 0.0, 0.0])
# Using Rodrigues' formula for 180 degrees (cos(π)=-1, sin(π)=0):
# R = I + sin(θ)K + (1-cos(θ))K² = I + 0*K + 2*K² = I + 2*K²
K = np.array([
    [0, -axis[2], axis[1]],
    [axis[2], 0, -axis[0]],
    [-axis[1], axis[0], 0]
])

print('\\nResult:', result)

Actually, wait. Let me double-check the mathematical fact. A 180-degree rotation around axis `n` maps vector `v` to `-v + 2(n·v)n`. 

For `v = [-x, -y, -z]` to map to `[x, y, z]`:
We want: `-[-x, -y, -z] + 2(n·[-x, -y, -z])n = [x, y, z]`
Which gives: `[x, y, z] + 2(n·[-x, -y, -z])n = [x, y, z]`
Therefore: `2(n·[-x, -y, -z])n = [0, 0, 0]`

This means either `n·[-x, -y, -z] = 0` OR `n = [0, 0, 0]`.

Since `n` is a unit vector, `n ≠ [0, 0, 0]`, so we must have `n·[-x, -y, -z] = 0`.

This means the rotation axis must be orthogonal to the vector being rotated.

But if `n ⊥ v`, then `n·v = 0`, so the rotation formula becomes:
`R(v) = -v + 2(0)n = -v`

This means a 180-degree rotation around any axis orthogonal to `v` maps `v` to `-v`.

So there IS no 180-degree rotation that maps `v` to `-(-v) = v`. The only way to map `v` to `v` is with a 0-degree or 360-degree rotation (identity).

This suggests there's a fundamental misunderstanding somewhere. import numpy as np

# Verify that original SVD approach gives correct alignment
first_vector = np.array([ 0.80473785,  0.50587936, -0.31061722])
last_vector = np.array([-0.80473785, -0.50587936,  0.31061722])

a = first_vector / np.linalg.norm(first_vector)
b = last_vector / np.linalg.norm(last_vector)

# Original SVD approach
svd_a = np.linalg.svd(a[:, np.newaxis])[0]
svd_b = np.linalg.svd(b[:, np.newaxis])[0]
rotation_reflection = svd_b.dot(svd_a.T)

print('SVD reflection result:')
result = np.dot(rotation_reflection, last_vector)
print('Result:', result)
print('Target: ', first_vector)
print('Error:', np.linalg.norm(result - first_vector))
print('Determinant:', np.linalg.det(rotation_reflection))

# This shows the SVD approach gives essentially perfect alignment
# but determinant = -1

# Now I need to find a rotation matrix that:
# 1. Has determinant = 1
# 2. Still approximately satisfies R*last_vector ≈ first_vector

import trimesh
import numpy as np

# Find which vector causes the 2.0 error
np.random.seed(0)
target = np.array([0, 0, -1], dtype=np.float64)
random_vectors = trimesh.unitize(np.random.random((1000, 3)) - .5)
basis_vectors = trimesh.util.generate_basis(target)
special_vectors = np.array([[7.12106798e-07, -7.43194705e-08, 1.00000000e+00], [0, 0, -1]])

vectors = np.vstack((random_vectors, [-target, target], basis_vectors, special_vectors))

align = trimesh.geometry.align_vectors

for i, vector in enumerate(vectors):
    T, a = align(vector, target, return_angle=True)
    check = np.dot(T[:3, :3], vector)
    norm = np.linalg.norm(check - target)
    if norm >= 1e-8:
        print(f'Error at index {i}:')
        print(f'  Vector: {vector}')
        print(f'  Check: {check}')
        print(f'  Target: {target}')
        print(f'  Error: {norm}')
        print(f'  Dot product: {np.dot(vector/np.linalg.norm(vector), target/np.linalg.norm(target))}')
        if norm > 1.5:  # This looks like the 2.0 error case
            break

import trimesh
import numpy as np

# Run with same seed as test and capture all errors
np.random.seed(0)
target = np.array([0, 0, -1], dtype=np.float64)
random_vectors = trimesh.unitize(np.random.random((1000, 3)) - .5)
basis_vectors = trimesh.util.generate_basis(target)
special_vectors = np.array([[7.12106798e-07, -7.43194705e-08, 1.00000000e+00], [0, 0, -1]])

vectors = np.vstack((random_vectors, [-target, target], basis_vectors, special_vectors))

align = trimesh.geometry.align_vectors

errors = []
for i, vector in enumerate(vectors):
    T, a = align(vector, target, return_angle=True)
    check = np.dot(T[:3, :3], vector)
    norm = np.linalg.norm(check - target)
    det = np.linalg.det(T)
    if norm >= 1e-8 or abs(det - 1.0) >= 1e-10:
        errors.append({
            'index': i,
            'vector': vector,
            'check': check,
            'target': target,
            'error': norm,
            'det': det,
            'dot': np.dot(vector/np.linalg.norm(vector), target/np.linalg.norm(target))
        })

print(f'Total errors: {len(errors)}')
for err in errors[:10]:  # Show first 10 errors
    print(f\"Index {err['index']}: error={err['error']:.6f}, det={err['det']:.6f}\")
    print(f\"  Vector: {err['vector']}\")
    print(f\"  Dot: {err['dot']}\")

import trimesh
import numpy as np

# Debug the transformation matrix
np.random.seed(0)
target = np.array([0, 0, -1], dtype=np.float64)
random_vectors = trimesh.unitize(np.random.random((1000, 3)) - .5)
basis_vectors = trimesh.util.generate_basis(target)
special_vectors = np.array([[7.12106798e-07, -7.43194705e-08, 1.00000000e+00], [0, 0, -1]])

vectors = np.vstack((random_vectors, [-target, target], basis_vectors, special_vectors))
problematic_vector = vectors[1000]

print('Problematic vector:', problematic_vector)
print('Target:', target)

# Manually compute what should happen
a = problematic_vector / np.linalg.norm(problematic_vector)
b = target / np.linalg.norm(target)

print('\\nUnit vectors:')
print('a:', a)
print('b:', b)
print('dot(a,b):', np.dot(a, b))

# Check if it triggers the special case
tol_merge = 1e-8
if np.dot(a, b) < (tol_merge - 1):
    print('Triggers special case (< -0.99999999)')
    
    # See what the SVD approach gives
    svd_a = np.linalg.svd(a[:, np.newaxis])[0]
    svd_b = np.linalg.svd(b[:, np.newaxis])[0]
    rotation_svd = svd_b.dot(svd_a.T)
    
    print('\\nSVD result:')
    print('Matrix:\\n', rotation_svd)
    print('det:', np.linalg.det(rotation_svd))
    print('SVD(a):', np.dot(rotation_svd, a))
    
    # Check what my fix does
    if np.linalg.det(rotation_svd) < 0:
        rotation_fixed = rotation_svd.copy()
        rotation_fixed[:, 2] *= -1
        print('\\nFixed result:')
        print('Matrix:\\n', rotation_fixed)
        print('det:', np.linalg.det(rotation_fixed))
        print('Fixed(a):', np.dot(rotation_fixed, a))
else:
    print('Does not trigger special case')

[Command finished with exit code 0]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>完美！现在我明白了问题所在：

1. **SVD 结果**：矩阵是 `[[1,0,0], [0,1,0], [0,0,-1]]`，行列式 = -1，结果 `SVD(a) = [0,0,-1]` ✅ 正确！
2. **我的修正结果**：矩阵是 `[[1,0,0], [0,1,0], [0,0,1]]`，行列式 = 1，结果 `Fixed(a) = [0,0,1]` ❌ 错误！

通过翻转第三列，我把一个正确的反射（将 `[0,0,1]` 映射到 `[0,0,-1]`）变成了一个恒等变换（将 `[0,0,1]` 映射到 `[0,0,1]`）。

我不能随便翻转一列。我需要一个更聪明的方法来将反射转换为旋转。

让我想想正确的数学方法。如果我有一个反射矩阵 R（det = -1）和一个旋转矩阵 R'（det = 1），它们都近似实现了相同的变换，那么我该如何选择？

在这种特殊情况下，由于输入向量是相反的，我需要的是一个 180 度的旋转。让我尝试构造正确的 180 度旋转矩阵。

对于将向量 v 映射到 -v 的 180 度旋转，旋转轴必须与 v 正交。让我用这种方法：<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/mikedh__trimesh__3.4/trimesh/geometry.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>    # are vectors just reversed
    if dot < (tol.merge - 1):
        # a reversed vector is 180 degrees
        angle = np.pi

        # https://github.com/mikedh/trimesh/issues/540
        svd_a = np.linalg.svd(a[:, np.newaxis])[0]
        svd_b = np.linalg.svd(b[:, np.newaxis])[0]
        rotation = svd_b.dot(svd_a.T)
        
        # ensure we have a proper rotation (determinant = 1)
        # not a reflection (determinant = -1)
        if np.linalg.det(rotation) < 0:
            # When vectors are nearly opposite, construct the correct 180-degree 
            # rotation matrix that maps 'a' to 'b' with determinant +1
            # 
            # For mapping vector 'a' to vector 'b' where b ≈ -a, we need a 
            # 180-degree rotation around an axis orthogonal to both vectors.
            # 
            # Find an axis orthogonal to 'a' (and therefore to 'b' since they're opposite)
            # Use the standard basis vector that is most orthogonal to 'a'
            basis_vectors = np.eye(3)
            best_axis_idx = 0
            min_abs_dot = 1.0
            
            for i in range(3):
                abs_dot = abs(np.dot(a, basis_vectors[i]))
                if abs_dot < min_abs_dot:
                    min_abs_dot = abs_dot
                    best_axis_idx = i
                    
            # Use the basis vector that's most orthogonal to 'a'
            axis = basis_vectors[best_axis_idx]
            
            # Construct 180-degree rotation matrix around this axis
            # Using Rodrigues' rotation formula for θ = π:
            # R = I + sin(π)*K + (1-cos(π))*K² = I + 0*K + 2*K² = I + 2*K²
            # where K is the cross-product matrix of the axis
            K = np.array([
                [0, -axis[2], axis[1]],
                [axis[2], 0, -axis[0]],
                [-axis[1], axis[0], 0]
            ])
            
            rotation = np.eye(3) + 2 * np.dot(K, K)</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>    # are vectors just reversed

        svd_b = np.linalg.svd(b[:, np.newaxis])[0]
        rotation = svd_b.dot(svd_a.T)
        
        # ensure we have a proper rotation (determinant = 1)
        # not a reflection (determinant = -1)
        if np.linalg.det(rotation) < 0:
            # Flip the sign of the third column to make determinant positive
            # This preserves the alignment property while ensuring proper rotation

   100	            
   101	            for i in range(3):
   102	                abs_dot = abs(np.dot(a, basis_vectors[i]))
   103	                if abs_dot < min_abs_dot:
   104	                    min_abs_dot = abs_dot
   105	                    best_axis_idx = i
   106	                    
   107	            # Use the basis vector that's most orthogonal to 'a'
   108	            axis = basis_vectors[best_axis_idx]
   109	            
   110	            # Construct 180-degree rotation matrix around this axis
   111	            # Using Rodrigues' rotation formula for θ = π:
   112	            # R = I + sin(π)*K + (1-cos(π))*K² = I + 0*K + 2*K² = I + 2*K²
   113	            # where K is the cross-product matrix of the axis
   114	            K = np.array([
   115	                [0, -axis[2], axis[1]],
   116	                [axis[2], 0, -axis[0]],
   117	                [-axis[1], axis[0], 0]
   118	            ])
   119	            
   120	            rotation = np.eye(3) + 2 * np.dot(K, K)
   121	
   122	    # are vectors already the same
   123	    elif dot > (1 - tol.merge):
   124	        angle = 0.0
   125	
Review the changes and make sure they are as expected. Edit the file again if necessary.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>现在让我测试这个修复：<tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/mikedh__trimesh__3.4 && python -c "

Comprehensive test for the align_vectors fix.

import trimesh
import numpy as np

def test_original_issue_cases():
    """Test the exact cases from the GitHub issue."""
    print("=== Testing Original Issue Cases ===")
    
    # Case 1: Working case from issue #540
    v1 = np.array([7.12106798e-07, -7.43194705e-08, 1.00000000e+00])
    c = trimesh.geometry.align_vectors([0,0,-1], v1)
    det_c = np.linalg.det(c)
    print(f"✓ Issue #540 case: det = {det_c:.15f}")
    assert abs(det_c - 1.0) < 1e-10, f"Determinant should be 1, got {det_c}"
    
    # Case 2: Failing case with tiny perturbations
    b = trimesh.geometry.align_vectors([0,0,-1], [-1e-17, 1e-17, 1])
    det_b = np.linalg.det(b)
    print(f"✓ Tiny perturbation case: det = {det_b:.15f}")
    assert abs(det_b - 1.0) < 1e-10, f"Determinant should be 1, got {det_b}"
    
    # Case 3: Failing case with larger perturbations  
    b2 = trimesh.geometry.align_vectors([0,0,-1], [-1e-4, 1e-4, 1])
    det_b2 = np.linalg.det(b2)
    print(f"✓ Larger perturbation case: det = {det_b2:.15f}")
    assert abs(det_b2 - 1.0) < 1e-10, f"Determinant should be 1, got {det_b2}"
    
    # Case 4: Working case (exact opposite)
    a = trimesh.geometry.align_vectors([0,0,-1], [0,0,1])
    det_a = np.linalg.det(a)
    print(f"✓ Exact opposite case: det = {det_a:.15f}")
    assert abs(det_a - 1.0) < 1e-10, f"Determinant should be 1, got {det_a}"

def test_alignment_accuracy():
    """Test that vectors are properly aligned."""
    print("\n=== Testing Alignment Accuracy ===")
    
    test_cases = [
        ([0, 0, -1], [0, 0, 1]),           # Exact opposite
        ([0, 0, -1], [-1e-17, 1e-17, 1]), # Tiny perturbation
        ([0, 0, -1], [-1e-4, 1e-4, 1]),   # Larger perturbation
        ([1, 0, 0], [-1, 0, 0]),          # X-axis opposite
        ([0, 1, 0], [0, -1, 0]),          # Y-axis opposite
        ([0, 0, 1], [0, 0, -1]),          # Z-axis opposite
    ]
    
    for i, (source, target) in enumerate(test_cases):
        transform = trimesh.geometry.align_vectors(source, target)
        det = np.linalg.det(transform)
        source_transformed = np.dot(transform[:3, :3], source)
        target_normalized = target / np.linalg.norm(target)
        alignment_error = np.linalg.norm(source_transformed - target_normalized)
        
        print(f"✓ Test case {i+1}: det = {det:.15f}, error = {alignment_error:.2e}")
        assert abs(det - 1.0) < 1e-10, f"Case {i+1}: Determinant should be 1, got {det}"
        assert alignment_error < 1e-10, f"Case {i+1}: Alignment error too large: {alignment_error}"

def test_various_angles():
    """Test various angles between vectors."""
    print("\n=== Testing Various Angles ===")
    
    # Generate vectors at different angles
    angles = np.linspace(0, np.pi, 10)
    for i, angle in enumerate(angles):
        # Create two unit vectors at the specified angle
        a = np.array([1.0, 0.0, 0.0])  # Reference vector
        b = np.array([np.cos(angle), np.sin(angle), 0.0])  # Vector at angle
        
        transform = trimesh.geometry.align_vectors(a, b)
        det = np.linalg.det(transform)
        a_transformed = np.dot(transform[:3, :3], a)
        alignment_error = np.linalg.norm(a_transformed - b)
        
        print(f"✓ Angle {np.degrees(angle):6.1f}°: det = {det:.15f}, error = {alignment_error:.2e}")
        assert abs(det - 1.0) < 1e-10, f"Angle {angle}: Determinant should be 1, got {det}"
        assert alignment_error < 1e-10, f"Angle {angle}: Alignment error too large: {alignment_error}"

def test_random_vectors():
    """Test with random vectors."""
    print("\n=== Testing Random Vectors ===")
    
    np.random.seed(42)  # For reproducibility
    for i in range(20):
        # Generate random unit vectors
        a = trimesh.unitize(np.random.random(3) - 0.5)
        b = trimesh.unitize(np.random.random(3) - 0.5)
        
        transform = trimesh.geometry.align_vectors(a, b)
        det = np.linalg.det(transform)
        a_transformed = np.dot(transform[:3, :3], a)
        alignment_error = np.linalg.norm(a_transformed - b)
        
        # Print occasional status updates
        if i % 5 == 0:
            print(f"✓ Random test {i}: det = {det:.15f}, error = {alignment_error:.2e}")
        
        assert abs(det - 1.0) < 1e-10, f"Random test {i}: Determinant should be 1, got {det}"
        assert alignment_error < 1e-10, f"Random test {i}: Alignment error too large: {alignment_error}"

def test_edge_cases():
    """Test edge cases and boundary conditions.""" 
    print("\n=== Testing Edge Cases ===")
    
    edge_cases = [
        # Nearly parallel vectors (should use different code path)
        ([1, 0, 0], [1, 1e-10, 0]),
        ([0, 1, 0], [0, 1, 1e-12]),
        
        # Nearly opposite vectors (our main fix)
        ([1, 0, 0], [-1, 1e-15, 0]),
        ([0, 1, 0], [0, -1, 1e-14]),
        
        # Very small vectors (after normalization)
        ([1e-100, 0, 0], [0, 1e-100, 0]),
        
        # Vectors with very large components
        ([1e10, 0, 0], [0, 1e10, 0]),
    ]
    
    for i, (a, b) in enumerate(edge_cases):
        try:
            transform = trimesh.geometry.align_vectors(a, b)
            det = np.linalg.det(transform)
            a_unit = a / np.linalg.norm(a)
            b_unit = b / np.linalg.norm(b)
            a_transformed = np.dot(transform[:3, :3], a_unit)
            alignment_error = np.linalg.norm(a_transformed - b_unit)
            
            print(f"✓ Edge case {i+1}: det = {det:.15f}, error = {alignment_error:.2e}")
            assert abs(det - 1.0) < 1e-8, f"Edge case {i+1}: Determinant should be 1, got {det}"
            assert alignment_error < 1e-6, f"Edge case {i+1}: Alignment error too large: {alignment_error}"
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"⚠ Edge case {i+1} failed with exception: {e}")
            # Some extreme edge cases might fail due to numerical issues, that's acceptable

if __name__ == "__main__":

import trimesh
import numpy as np

# Debug the specific case
source = np.array([0, 0, -1])
target = np.array([-1e-4, 1e-4, 1])

a = source / np.linalg.norm(source)
b = target / np.linalg.norm(target)

print('Unit vectors:')
print('a:', a)
print('b:', b)
print('dot(a,b):', np.dot(a, b))

# This should use the normal cross-product path, not the special case
# But it's triggering the special case because of floating point precision

# Let's see what the normal path would give
dot = np.dot(a, b)
if dot < (1e-8 - 1):
    print('Triggers special case')
elif dot > (1 - 1e-8):
    print('Triggers identical case') 
    rotation = np.eye(3)
else:
    print('Triggers normal case')
    # Normal cross-product approach
    angle = np.arccos(dot)
    w = np.cross(a, b)
    c = 1.0 / (1.0 + dot)
    wx = np.array([[0, -w[2], w[1]],
                   [w[2], 0, -w[0]],
                   [-w[1], w[0], 0]])
    rotation = np.eye(3) + wx + (np.dot(wx, wx) * c)
    print('Normal rotation det:', np.linalg.det(rotation))
    result = np.dot(rotation, a)
    print('Normal result:', result)
    print('Target:', b)
    print('Normal error:', np.linalg.norm(result - b))
"</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>cd /workspace/mikedh__trimesh__3.4 && python -c "

# Debug the specific case
source = np.array([0, 0, -1])
target = np.array([-1e-4, 1e-4, 1])
a = source / np.linalg.norm(source)
b = target / np.linalg.norm(target)
print('Unit vectors:')
print('a:', a)
print('b:', b)
print('dot(a,b):', np.dot(a, b))
# This should use the normal cross-product path, not the special case
# But it's triggering the special case because of floating point precision
# Let's see what the normal path would give
dot = np.dot(a, b)
if dot < (1e-8 - 1):
    print('Triggers special case')
elif dot > (1 - 1e-8):
    print('Triggers identical case')
    rotation = np.eye(3)
else:
    print('Triggers normal case')
    # Normal cross-product approach
    angle = np.arccos(dot)
    w = np.cross(a, b)
    c = 1.0 / (1.0 + dot)
    wx = np.array([[0, -w[2], w[1]],
                   [w[2], 0, -w[0]],
                   [-w[1], w[0], 0]])
    rotation = np.eye(3) + wx + (np.dot(wx, wx) * c)
    print('Normal rotation det:', np.linalg.det(rotation))
    result = np.dot(rotation, a)
    print('Normal result:', result)
    print('Target:', b)
    print('Normal error:', np.linalg.norm(result - b))
